 1 Purpose
The purpose of this Environmental Calculation File (ECF) is to expand the representation of the 216-B-3
Pond (B Pond) system for the Soil Inventory Model (SIM), which tracks past liquid discharges and the
inventory of radionuclides and chemicals at over 400 waste sites located in the Central Plateau of the
Hanford Site. This calculation performs the partitioning of historical B Pond system liquid discharge
volumes and inventory in time and space among the influent ditches, ponds, and associated pond lobes,
consistent with the infiltration distribution methodology documented in ECF-HANFORD-19-0032,
Distribution of Infiltration in the 216-U-10, 216-B-3 Pond, and 216-T-4 Pond Systems (1944 – 1997). This
increased resolution allows models to explicitly account for spatiotemporal variations within the B Pond
complex that were not previously represented in the SIM.

The updated inventory calculations were performed using SIMv2.3 (ECF-HANFORD-24-0098, Updates to
the Hanford Soil Inventory Model (SIM Version 2) for FY 2025). SIMv2.3 was run only for the additional
B Pond sites developed as part of this calculation. The information developed in this ECF will provide the
technical basis for incorporating the expanded B Pond system representation into a future SIM release,
which will allow downstream users to incorporate the B Pond system inventories at a higher resolution.

1.1 Document Organization
This ECF is organized as follows:
• Chapter 2 provides background on the B Pond system and the waste site discharge fractionation
 documented in ECF-HANFORD-19-0032.
• Chapter 3 provides the methodology behind the implementation of B Pond system fractionation into
 the SIM.
• Chapter 4 provides the assumptions and inputs for the B Pond system fractionation.
• Chapter 5 provides documentation on the approved software used in the calculations.
• Chapter 6 provides the calculation involved with implementing B Pond system fractionation into the
 SIM.
• Chapter 7 provides the results and conclusions of the B Pond system fractionation implementation.
• Chapter 8 provides the references for the document.

 2 Background
The B Pond system is located east of the 200 East Area and consists of the main 216-B-3 Pond and
three expansion lobes: 216-B-3A, 216-B-3B, and 216-B-3C (WHC-EP-0367, Liquid Effluent Study Final
Project Report). Figure 1 shows the spatial relationship of the main 216-B-3 Pond, the three expansion
lobes, and the associated influent ditch system east of the 200 East Area. The B Pond system received
approximately 2.8 × 10^8 m^3 of wastewater between 1945 and 1997 (ECF-HANFORD-17-0079, Hanford
Soil Inventory Model (SIM-v2) Calculated Radionuclide Inventory of Direct Liquid Discharges to Soil in the
Hanford Site's 200 Areas). ECF-HANFORD-19-0032 evaluated the historical configuration of the B Pond
system and developed the temporal and spatial distribution of infiltration among the individual pond
components.

The individual components of the B Pond system operated during different and, in some cases,
overlapping periods. The main 216-B-3 Pond operated from April 1945 through April 1994. Expansion
lobe 216-B-3A operated from October 1983 through April 1994, except from February through May 1984.
Expansion lobe 216-B-3B received effluent only during January 1984 following failure of the dike between
216-B-3A and 216-B-3B. Expansion lobe 216-B-3C began operating during 1985 and remained in
operation through August 1997. Table 1 summarizes the periods during which each component received
effluent. Table 2 presents the discharge surface areas. The operating periods summarized in Table 1 and
the discharge surface areas summarized in Table 2 provide the temporal and spatial information used to
partition the historical B Pond system discharge volumes.

 3

 3 Methodology
The partitioning of the discharge volume from B Pond to these new sites is based on calculations
performed in ECF-HANFORD-19-0032 where the fraction of the total discharge that infiltrates from a
particular B Pond site is the ratio of the area of that site to the total area of all B Pond sites active during
that period (Table 1). The discharge surface area for each B Pond site is shown in Table 2.

Because the configuration of the B Pond system changed during several calendar years, the routing
calculations also account for partial-year operation of the individual pond lobes. The annual discharge
volume is first apportioned according to the fraction of the year during which a particular B Pond
configuration was active and is then distributed among the active sites based on their relative discharge
surface areas. For periods when only one B Pond site was active, that site receives the entire discharge
volume for that portion of the year. Table 3 presents the resulting flow-routing equations used to partition
the annual B Pond discharge volumes.

The fractional splits shown in Table 3 for a given waste site represent the monthly allocation of volumes
within a given discharge year. For example, the 1984 routing equations account for three different B Pond
system configurations during the year. During January, the annual discharge is apportioned among
216-B-3, 216-B-3A, and 216-B-3B based on their relative discharge surface areas. From February
through May, only 216-B-3 was active and therefore receives the corresponding four-twelfths of the
annual discharge. From June through December, the remaining seven-twelfths of the annual discharge is
apportioned between 216-B-3 and 216-B-3A based on their relative discharge surface areas.

 Table 3. Flow Routing Equations for Calculating the New Waste Discharge Volumes for B Pond Sites
 Years Flow Routing Equations
 1945 through 1982 216-B-3: Receives 100% of the total volume
                       216-B-3: (9/12) x total volume + (3/12) x total volume x 216-B-3 area / (216-B-3 area +
        1983           216-B-3A area).
                       216-B-3A: (3/12) x total volume x 216-B-3A area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area).
                       216-B-3: (1/12) x total volume x 216-B-3 area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area + 216-B-3B
                       area) + (4/12) x total volume + (7/12) x total volume x 216-B-3 area / (216-B-3 area +
                       216-B-3A area).
        1984           216-B-3A: (1/12) x total volume x 216-B-3A area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area +
                       216-B-3B area) + (7/12) x total volume x 216-B-3A area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area).
                       216-B-3B: (1/12) x total volume x 216-B-3B area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area +
                       216-B-3B area).
                       216-B-3: total volume x 216-B-3 area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area + 216-B-3C area).
 1985 through 1993     216-B-3A: total volume x 216-B-3A area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area + 216-B-3C area).
                       216-B-3C: total volume x 216-B-3C area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area + 216-B-3C area).
                       216-B-3: (4/12) x total volume x 216-B-3 area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area + 216-B-3C
 area).
                       216-B-3A: (4/12) x total volume x 216-B-3A area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area +
 1994
                       216-B-3C area).
                       216-B-3C: (4/12) x total volume x 216-B-3C area / (216-B-3 area + 216-B-3A area +
                       216-B-3C area) + (8/12)x total volume.
 1995 through 1997 216-B-3C: Receives 100% of the total volume
Source: ECF-HANFORD-19-0032, Distribution of Infiltration in the 216-U-10, 216-B-3 Pond, and 216-T-4 Pond Systems
1944-1997.

 4

 4 Assumptions and Inputs
The SIMv2.3 input workbook (SIMv2.3_Input.xlsx) was used as the basis for developing the B Pond site
inputs. Three additional sites, 216-B-3A, 216-B-3B, and 216-B-3C, were added to represent the B Pond
expansion lobes. The volumetric discharge parameters for the existing 216-B-3 site and the three
additional sites were developed using the flow-routing methodology described in Chapter 3 and the
discharge surface areas and operating periods presented in Chapter 2. The resulting volumetric input
parameters are provided in Appendix A.

The additional B Pond sites retain the waste-stream assignments used for the existing 216-B-3 site. The
relative proportions of the waste streams were maintained when the annual 216-B-3 discharge volumes
were partitioned among the B Pond sites. No changes were made to the waste-stream concentration,
density, or uncertainty distributions as part of this calculation.

The existing 216-B-3 site is assigned to the 200-CW-1 operable unit in SIM (ECF-HANFORD-17-0079).
Because 216-B-3A, 216-B-3B, and 216-B-3C represent spatial subdivisions of the same B Pond
discharge and retain the same waste-stream assignments, the additional sites were also assigned to the
200-CW-1 operable unit for SIM output. The resulting inventories are reported in the 200-CW-1 output
workbooks.

 5 Software Applications
In accordance with CPCC-PRO-IRM-309, Controlled Software Management, software applications fit one
of two categories: spreadsheets or utility calculation. Software programs used for this ECF are Microsoft
Excel and GoldSim version 15.0. Microsoft ® Excel® was used as the utility tool for calculations and
organizing the input tables. All model input files, selected output files, and other relevant files for the
development of this ECF are archived to the Hanford Site's Environmental Model Management Archive
(EMMA) internal file directory referenced under this ECF number and revision (ECF-HANFORD-26-0027,
Rev. 0) in accordance with requirements of Central Plateau Cleanup Company's quality assurance
modeling project plan.

5.1 GoldSim
The approved utility calculation software used in this ECF, which complies with requirements of
CPCC-PRO-IRM-309, is GoldSim ®. CP-68009, GoldSim: Version 15 Software Management Plan,
provides details on the applicable software quality assurance documentation. The use of GoldSim
version 15.0 at the Hanford Site is managed and controlled such that the computational needs filled by
use of GoldSim (and any associated utility codes) and the specific roles and responsibilities for
management, the modeling staff, and subcontractors have been identified and traced.
• Software title: GoldSim
• Software version: Version 15

5.2 Support Software
• Microsoft Excel – Used to store/edit input and output data for the SIM. Input data include waste
 stream properties (analyte concentrations and waste density) and volumetric discharge/distribution to
 SIM sites. Output data include analyte inventory for each site (2001 decayed and back-decayed) and
 total volume discharge.

 6 Calculation
The distribution of the waste volume per year to the new and existing SIM sites for the B Pond system will
be added to the SIM in the next SIM version update. For pond system sites that already exist (e.g.,
216-B-3), the annual discharge volume assigned to each site is calculated based on the routing equations
shown previously in Table 3 and the surface areas for each discharge site shown previously in Table 2.
The resulting updated B Pond system volumetric distribution on an annual basis is presented in Table 4.

B Pond: 216-B-3 Pond

Consistent with the operating history of the B Pond system, 216-B-3 receives the full annual discharge
prior to operation of the expansion lobes. Beginning in 1983, the annual discharge is distributed among
the B Pond sites that were active during each year. Site 216-B-3B receives a discharge only in 1984,
while 216-B-3C begins receiving discharge in 1985 and receives the full annual B Pond discharge after
closure of 216-B-3 and 216-B-3A. The site-specific volumes for each year collectively represent the
historical annual B Pond discharge redistributed among the applicable pond sites.

The annual site volumes presented in Table 4 were used to develop the individual waste-stream volume
records for 216-B-3, 216-B-3A, 216-B-3B, and 216-B-3C. For each discharge year, the waste streams
associated with the existing 216-B-3 site were distributed among the applicable B Pond sites while
maintaining the relative proportions of the individual waste streams. The resulting site- and waste stream-
specific volumetric input parameters are provided in Appendix A. The resulting B Pond input records were
implemented in SIMv2.3 to calculate the inventories associated with the expanded B Pond site
representation. SIMv2.3 was run for the B Pond sites addressed by this calculation; a full SIMv2.3
inventory update was not performed. The calculated B Pond inputs and inventories documented in this
ECF provide the basis for incorporation of these sites into the next SIM version update.

 7

 7 Results/Conclusions
The volumetric distribution calculations developed in ECF-HANFORD-19-0032 and listed in Table 3 were
applied to the historical B Pond system discharge volumes to develop revised volumetric discharge
parameters for sites 216-B-3, 216-B-3A, 216-B-3B, and 216-B-3C in the SIM input file
(SIMv2.3_Input.xlsx). The revised volumetric parameters for the B Pond sites are presented in
Appendix A.

These volumetric parameters were implemented in SIMv2.3 so that the site- and waste stream-specific
discharge volumes could be combined with the existing SIM waste-stream density and analyte
concentration parameters to calculate the corresponding inventories. The resulting chemical inventory
means and standard deviations are presented in Appendix B, and the corresponding radionuclide
inventory means and standard deviations are presented in Appendix C. The volumetric inputs and
calculated inventories documented in this ECF provide the technical basis for incorporating the expanded
B Pond site representation into the next SIM update.

SIMv2.3 was run only for the B Pond sites addressed by this calculation; a full SIMv2.3 inventory update
was not performed.
